However, our extensive evaluation of ten methods across three MLLM families and five ability groups reveals a critical gap: strong general performance often comes at the cost of severe degradation on fine-grained tasks such as OCR and visual grounding. We trace these failures to two limitations of the prevailing paradigm of retaining the highest-ranked native patches. A single importance ranking overlooks the complementary evidence required by different tasks, while sparse patch selection disrupts both image content and spatial integrity. To address these limitations, we propose Bifocal, a training-free, two-stage pruning method built on a mixed-granularity framework. It allocates separate budgets to query-focused and image-intrinsic evidence, complemented by a pooled coarse representation of the entire image. Before the LLM, Bifocal matches question words to visual regions through a soft conjunction balanced against norm-debiased image saliency, while selecting image-intrinsic evidence to maximize representational coverage. Inside the LLM, it further prunes tokens using density-adjusted text-to-image attention. With roughly a quarter of the original visual tokens, Bifocal is the only evaluated method to retain over 90% of unpruned performance in every ability group across all three models, and ranks first on average in all nine model-budget settings. The code is available at: https://github.com/EIT-NLP/Bifocal

</details>


### [330] [Resource-Efficient 3D Shape and Orientation Estimation of Road Users using Roadside RGB Camera and 3D Radar](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18417)
*Shiva Agrawal,Savankumar Bhanderi,Gordon Elger*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: 提出一种针对路侧相机-雷达融合的两阶段3D目标检测方法：先用现成2D框与雷达点提取，再用轻量模块用雷达定位置、模板定尺寸、小网络预测方位，实现实时、低成本、易迁移。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 车辆载传感器几何一致性高，端到端模型易泛化；但路侧部署高度/俯仰/视场差异大，端到端需要大量站点特定数据，适配困难、成本高，亟需可快速迁移、资源友好的3D感知方案。

Method: 两阶段管线：阶段一用现成SOTA相机检测器得到2D框，并关联每个目标的3D雷达点；阶段二构建3D定向包围盒：用雷达数据回归3D位置，用类别预定义尺寸模板设定大小，以一个极小神经网络学习站点特定的偏航角（yaw）优化。

Result: 在带有弯道与路口的路侧场景，对行人/自行车/汽车验证，部署于NVIDIA Jetson ORIN达25 FPS，满足实时性；能稳健输出3D形状与朝向。

Conclusion: 相较复杂端到端模型，该方法以更低数据与算力成本实现路侧3D检测的可迁移与实时部署；通过模板+雷达+小网络分解问题，规避了站点变化带来的泛化难题，适合多站点快速落地。

Abstract: End-to-end deep learning pipelines for camera-radar 3D object detection are well established in driver assistance systems and autonomous vehicles; however, adapting them to roadside-mounted infrastructure presents significant challenges. On vehicles, sensor heights, pitch angles, and relative geometries remain largely uniform, allowing end-to-end models to generalize effectively. On the contrary, roadside sensor installations vary drastically in height, tilt, and field of view across locations, rendering end-to-end models prone to failure and difficult to adapt without massive location-specific datasets. Hence, instead of designing a complex end-to-end pipeline for direct 3D bounding box estimation, a low-cost, resource-efficient two-stage approach is proposed for real-time 3D shape and orientation estimation. The first stage utilizes any off-the-shelf, state-of-the-art method to extract 2D camera bounding boxes and associated 3D radar points for each road user. The proposed second stage then processes these inputs to construct 3D oriented bounding boxes where radar data determines 3D position, predefined templates define size for each road user, and a tiny neural network predicts a site-specific and optimized yaw angle. The algorithm is successfully validated at a curved road with a junction for pedestrians, bicycles, and cars, where it achieves 25 frames per second on an NVIDIA Jetson ORIN for real-time applications.

</details>


### [331] [CoTL: Compact Token Learning for 3D Scene Representation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18446)
*Han Liang,Sibo Wu,Anpei Chen,Rüdiger Westermann*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: CoTL 提出一种从无位姿多视图图像中学习紧凑token集合以保留场景几何与外观的框架；通过压缩编码与查询式高斯解码，实现可扩展、分辨率无关的场景表示，并在新视角合成与生成任务上取得竞争性表现。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有多视图场景表示常需已知位姿、特征体素或高容量特征，难以在观测数变化时保持紧凑与可扩展；需要一种既能从无位姿数据中学习，又能以小型表示保留可渲染3D信息的方法，以支持下游生成与重建。

Method: 1) 编码器将每张输入图像压缩为少量潜在token；2) 跨视角聚合使这些token形成视角一致的场景表示；每增加一视角仅添加小而与分辨率无关的token集；3) 使用基于查询的高斯解码器：场景无关的查询对token做注意力，生成显式、可渲染的3D高斯元；4) 通过新视角渲染监督端到端训练整个紧凑表示与解码器。

Result: 在 retenk{} 与 ACID 数据集上，CoTL 的紧凑、非结构化token集实现了具有竞争力的新视角合成表现，并可作为3D高斯场景生成模型的潜在空间。

Conclusion: 以3D高斯重建为目标能有效驱动紧凑场景表示学习；所学token空间具备扩展性与通用性，可作为3D视觉更广泛下游任务的基础。

Abstract: We present CoTL, a framework that learns a compact set of tokens that preserves scene geometry and appearance from unposed multi-view images. Our encoder compresses the input images into small per-view sets of latent tokens that aggregate information across views to form a view-consistent scene representation. Each additional view expands the representation with only a small, resolution-independent set of tokens, allowing its capacity to grow with the available observations. To force this compact bottleneck to retain sufficient scene information, a query-based Gaussian decoder uses scene-independent queries to attend to the learned tokens and recover an explicit, renderable set of 3D Gaussian primitives. Novel-view rendering supervision trains the compact representation end to end through the reconstructed Gaussian scene. Experiments on \retenk{} and ACID show that the resulting compact, unstructured token set supports competitive novel-view synthesis and can serve as the latent space of a generative model for 3D Gaussian scene generation. Together, these results show that 3D Gaussian reconstruction is an effective objective for compact scene representation learning and the learned token space provides a basis for broader downstream tasks in 3D vision.

</details>


### [332] [EmoGenie: Benchmarking and Evaluating Expressed and Evoked Emotion in T2I Generation with MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18455)
*Zixuan Xing,Lancheng Gao,Shengyan Li,Huiyu Duan,Zhaolin Cai,Guangtao Zhai,Xiongkuo Min*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: EmoGenie 构建了针对AI生成图像的情感基准（表达与唤起两维），并提出模型EmoProbe，在情感问答与连续情感/美学评分上达SOTA相关性。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有情感基准多基于自然图像，且对生成图像的研究多局限于人像表达；T2I图像的情感常偏离目标，MLLM能否可靠解读生成图像情感尚不清楚，缺乏统一数据与评测框架。

Method: 构建EmoGenie：由8个T2I模型用800个情感定向提示生成6400张图。标注两部分：1) EmoGenie-QA：9万条封闭式问答（单图/图对）+1057条开放式描述；2) EmoGenie-Assess：基于人类评分的连续指标（情感维度V-A-D与美学）共13175个MOS。提出EmoProbe：将情感QA与维度化回归（对齐回答、面向V/A/D与美学的回归头）联合，实现从粗到细的统一情感理解。

Result: 在情感相关QA任务上，EmoProbe优于现有方法；在V/A/D与美学的连续预测上，与人类评分的相关性更高，整体表现达SOTA。

Conclusion: EmoGenie为AI生成图像情感研究提供了统一的基准与评测协议；EmoProbe证明了将问答与维度回归联合可提升对生成图像情感（表达与唤起）与美学的细粒度理解。

Abstract: Rapid advances in artificial intelligence-generated content (AIGC) have significantly promoted using text-to-image (T2I) models to generate images with specific affective intentions. However, the emotional effects conveyed by generated images frequently deviate from target expectations, and it remains unclear whether multimodal large language models (MLLMs) can reliably interpret the emotion of AI-generated visual content. Existing affective benchmarks are predominantly built on natural images, while affective studies of generated images have largely focused on emotions expressed in portraits, lacking a unified benchmark for studying the emotions expressed and evoked by generated images. We therefore introduce EmoGenie, a benchmark of emotion for AI-generated images that evaluates capabilities in T2I generation and MLLM understanding across expressed and evoked emotions. It comprises 6,400 images generated by eight T2I models using 800 emotion-targeted prompts, annotated with question-answer (QA) pairs, termed EmoGenie-QA, and continuous human ratings, termed EmoGenie-Assess. EmoGenie-QA contains 90,000 closed-set questions in single-image and image-pair settings, as well as 1,057 open-ended questions for emotion description. EmoGenie-Assess benchmarks continuous affective and aesthetic prediction using 13,175 mean opinion scores (MOSs) aggregated from human ratings in terms of valence, arousal, dominance, and aesthetic quality. We further develop EmoProbe, which combines emotion question answering with response-aligned, dimension-specific regression heads for continuous score prediction. Experimental results show that EmoProbe achieves superior results on emotion-related QA tasks, while its affective and aesthetic predictions correlate better with human ratings, demonstrating its state-of-the-art capabilities of unified coarse-to-fine affective understanding for AI-generated images.

</details>


### [333] [$\mathrm{S}^3$-PnP: Plug-and-Play Denoising for Scientific Imaging with Self-Supervised Score Priors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18458)
*Yukun Cui,Jiangshe Zhang,Shuang Xu,Lilun Deng,Haowen Bai,Tao Feng,Haotong Qin,Konrad Schindler,Zixiang Zhao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: 提出S^3-PnP：无需干净标注与外部预训练，用自监督方式在PnP-ADMM中直接从观测诱导的中间状态学习score先验，并在HSI/MRI等科学成像中优于现有方法。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 科学成像(如HSI、MRI)难以获得配对的干净目标，导致大规模有监督训练受限；直接用外部预训练扩散先验的PnP-ADMM存在两类失配：先验与目标分布不匹配、以及ADMM中由对偶反馈导致的去噪器输入分布不匹配。

Method: 构建自监督PnP框架S^3-PnP：不依赖干净标签或外部权重，从观测数据在ADMM迭代产生的中间变量中学习score函数；该score用于修正对解析先验更新的输入，将观测驱动的校正与解析正则项结合；并在二次数据保真下分析校正ADMM的收敛性与重建误差（在理想score假设）。

Result: 在参考引导HSI复原、单幅HSI去噪、MRI去噪上，相比竞争方法取得一致性能提升。

Conclusion: 自监督地在算法内学习与PnP-ADMM统计一致的score先验可缓解分布失配，带来稳定收敛与更优重建；为无干净标注的科学成像提供有效替代方案。

Abstract: Modern image restoration methods increasingly rely on large-scale supervised training, yet clean targets are often prohibitively difficult to obtain in scientific imaging modalities such as hyperspectral imaging (HSI) and magnetic resonance imaging (MRI). Plug-and-play ADMM (PnP-ADMM) with pretrained diffusion priors offers a promising alternative, but the learned prior distribution can mismatch both the target scientific image distribution and the distribution of ADMM denoiser inputs induced by dual feedback. To mitigate these mismatches, we propose $\mathrm{S}^3$-PnP, a self-supervised plug-and-play restoration framework that learns a score prior directly from observation-derived ADMM intermediate states, without clean training targets or externally pretrained weights. Specifically, the learned score corrects the input to the analytical prior update, combining observation-driven corrections with analytical regularization. We further analyze the convergence and reconstruction error of the corrected ADMM algorithm assuming an ideal score and quadratic data fidelity. Experiments on reference-guided HSI restoration, single-image HSI denoising, and MRI denoising demonstrate consistent improvements over competing methods.

</details>


### [334] [MFVSR: A Multi-Style and Fine-Grained Benchmark for Video Subtitle Removal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18461)
*Xiyan Huang,Qingdong He,Peng Tang,Bo Yin,Chaoyi Wang,Xiaobin Hu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: 提出MFVSR，一个用于视频字幕去除（VSR）的多风格大规模细粒度基准，覆盖多源高/低清与AI生成视频、16种语言与多样字幕属性，并引入信息条件化、空间解耦与盲/掩码两类评测协议，分别衡量恢复、保真、时序一致、覆盖率与失败；实验证明传统整帧指标掩盖了关键差异，MFVSR提供更可靠与诊断性的评估基础。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有VSR评测存在数据分辨率低、测试集零散、沿用通用视频修复协议且无法捕捉局部恢复错误、非目标篡改、时序不稳与输出不完整等问题，导致方法优劣与可靠性难以客观反映。

Method: 构建MFVSR基准：1）数据层面，收集/合成3000对配对视频（Real-HQ、Real-LQ、AI生成三源），并提供953条真实野外视频用于覆盖性分析；2）字幕要素受控，覆盖16种语言与四大因子（空间布局、时间行为、视觉风格、语言模式）；3）评测协议层面，提出信息条件化、空间解耦评测，区分盲去除与掩码引导，分项度量恢复保真、非字幕区域保真、时间保真、覆盖率与失败率。

Result: 在六种代表性方法上实验显示：局部恢复质量、字幕外区域的内容保持、时间一致性与整体可靠性之间存在显著差异，而这些差异被传统整帧指标所掩盖。

Conclusion: MFVSR提供更全面、可诊断、可靠性感知的VSR评测框架，为未来字幕去除方法的发展和公平比较奠定基础。

Abstract: Video subtitle removal (VSR) is an important video restoration task, yet existing evaluation remains limited by low-resolution datasets, fragmented test sets, and generic video-inpainting protocols that fail to adequately capture localized restoration errors, unintended modifications, temporal instability, and incomplete outputs. In this paper, we introduce MFVSR, a large-scale multi-style benchmark for fine-grained and reliability-aware video subtitle removal evaluation. MFVSR distinguishes itself through three key features: (1) Large-scale and diverse data, comprising 3,000 paired videos from Real-HQ, Real-LQ, and AI-Generated sources, with an auxiliary set of 953 in-the-wild real videos for real-world coverage analysis; (2) Controlled subtitle diversity, spanning 16 languages and four factors covering spatial layout, temporal behavior, visual style, and language mode; (3) Fine-grained evaluation, introducing an information-conditioned, spatially decoupled protocol that separates blind from mask-guided removal and measures Restoration Fidelity, Preservation Fidelity, Temporal Fidelity, coverage, and failures. Experiments on six representative methods reveal substantial discrepancies between local restoration, outside-region preservation, temporal consistency, and full-set reliability that are obscured by conventional full-frame metrics. MFVSR therefore provides a more comprehensive and diagnostic foundation for evaluating and advancing video subtitle removal methods.

</details>


### [335] [Simplifying and Improving On-Policy Distillation for Few-Step Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18472)
*Jiawei Zhang,Ziyu Wan,Nanye Ma,Long Zhao,Dong Chen,Hongyu Liu,Qifeng Chen,Dongdong Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: 提出一种名为SMD（Simplified Matching Distillation）的简化匹配蒸馏方法，通过在策略速度回归与训练期“Pivot”残差代理，实现4步（4-NFE）高质量生成，超越多步教师与强基线，并支持跨模型/多教师/因果视频等场景。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有少步蒸馏方法常依赖额外的分数网络、对抗训练或特化架构，增加实现与推理复杂度；同时在少步（如4-NFE）场景下，如何高效刻画教师在粗时间间隔内的速度变化并避免差教师模式仍是挑战。

Method: 1) 以简单的on-policy速度回归为核心，让学生直接回归教师给定轨迹上的速度；2) 设计训练期使用的Pivot残差代理：将学生对每个粗间隔的区间平均速度预测与本地校正解耦，以表征教师在该间隔内的细粒度变化；3) 引入“远离弱教师”的排斥项，轻量避免不良模式；4) 全流程保持回归范式，无需在线分数估计或对抗训练，部署时生成器结构与推理过程不变。

Result: 在图像生成与编辑、3D生成、视频合成、以及音视频联合生成等任务上（规模至330亿参数），在4-NFE下优于强力少步基线，并在若干基准上超过多步教师；同时实现跨模型蒸馏、多教师蒸馏与因果视频蒸馏。

Conclusion: SMD以极简的回归式蒸馏框架，在无需额外分数模型或对抗机制的前提下，实现高效少步采样与高保真生成；其Pivot与弱教师排斥设计有效捕捉区间内动态并规避劣质模式，方法通用、可扩展、且部署零改动。

Abstract: Few-step distillation reduces the sampling cost of diffusion and flow models, but existing approaches often require auxiliary score models or specialized architectures. We show that simple on-policy velocity regression already provides a strong foundation for 4-NFE generation. To represent teacher velocity variation within each coarse transition, we introduce Pivot, a training-only residual proxy that separates local corrections from the student's interval-mean prediction. We further repel the student from weaker teacher predictions, providing a lightweight mechanism for avoiding undesirable modes. The resulting framework, Simplified Matching Distillation (SMD), remains entirely regression-based, requires neither online score estimation nor adversarial training, and leaves the deployed generator unchanged. We evaluate SMD on image generation and editing, 3D generation, video synthesis, and joint audio--video generation, scaling to 33 billion parameters. At 4-NFE, SMD outperforms strong few-step baselines and surpasses multi-step teachers on several benchmarks. It also supports cross-model, multi-teacher, and causal-video distillation.

</details>


### [336] [Physics-Aware Deepfake Detection via Distance-Speech Consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18485)
*Kyeongrae Kim,Kim Sung-Bin,Oh Hyun-Bin,Tae-Hyun Oh*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: 提出一种物理约束感知的动态说话人视频深伪检测方法，通过视频估计人与摄像机距离并与语音能量的距离依赖关系进行一致性校验，以弥补唇形同步在动态场景下失效的问题；在野外数据集上有效，并与唇同步检测器互补，集成后稳定提升。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有大多数音视深伪检测依赖唇-语音同步，通常假设静态、正面视角；在真实动态场景中，人物移动、口型遮挡或退化会导致这类方法不可靠，迫切需要不依赖唇部可见度的鲁棒线索。真实录制中语音声压级随人与相机距离变化而遵循物理规律，而生成模型可能破坏这种耦合，这是可利用的区分信号。

Method: 提出一种“物理感知”管线：1) 从视频估计说话人到相机的时变距离（例如基于人脸/头部尺度或场景几何的距离回归）；2) 从音频提取与距离相关的声学量（如短时能量/SPL、带通能量衰减、近讲效应指标等），建模其与估计距离的函数关系；3) 计算音-视一致性偏差作为深伪得分；4) 与传统唇同步检测器进行简单集成（ensemble）以提升鲁棒性。

Result: 在“in-the-wild”动态基准上，物理线索在人物移动、口型不清或缺失的情况下仍表现有效；与唇同步方法互补，简单集成在多个数据集上带来一致性能增益。

Conclusion: 利用真实世界的声学-几何耦合作为物理约束，可在动态场景中稳健检测说话人深伪；该思路独立于口型可见度，并能与现有唇同步检测器协同提升可靠性。代码已开源（PADD）。

Abstract: Recent progress in generative models has made talking-head deepfakes highly realistic and easy to create, jeopardizing public trust. However, most audio-visual deepfake detectors rely on lip-speech synchronization and are designed for static, frontal videos, limiting their reliability in dynamic, in-the-wild recordings where the speaker moves and lip cues are degraded or missing. As an alternative, we propose a physics-aware detector for dynamic speaking videos that leverages an acoustic constraint: in real recordings, speech energy varies predictably with speaker-to-camera distance, whereas deepfake generation can break this coupling. Our method combines distance estimates from video with distance-dependent acoustic measures from speech to detect deepfakes. Experiments on in-the-wild benchmarks show that the proposed physical cue is effective in dynamic scenarios, and that it complements lip-sync-based detectors, with a simple ensemble achieving consistent gains across datasets. Codes are available at https://byulharang.github.io/PADD/.

</details>


### [337] [EgoOmniWorld: Unifying Egocentric Understanding via Ego-Action-World Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18499)
*Songruo Chen,Yuqian Fu,Yu Li,Mingkang Dong,Sifan Zhou,Bin Ren,Xu Zheng,Tianwen Qian,Mohamed Elhoseiny,Yanwei Fu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Egocentric video understanding spans diverse capabilities, modalities, and evaluation formats, yet these are often studied in isolation despite their tight coupling in real-world interaction. We propose the Ego-Action-World interaction loop as a unifying principle: the wearer perceives the world, forms goals, acts, changes the world, and uses feedback to guide subsequent behavior. Based on this view, we introduce EgoOmniWorld, a benchmark organized around four complementary interaction structures: Node captures ego, action, and world states; Edge captures directed relations among them; Chain captures linked dependencies; and Loop captures feedback-closed interactions. We develop an interaction-first, evidence-grounded data pipeline that extracts structured interactions from egocentric videos, grounds them in visual and auditory evidence, and generates questions according to their interaction structure. Required modalities and single- or multi-turn formats follow naturally from the supporting evidence and dependencies. EgoOmniWorld contains 24,166 QA groups, including 20,284 training and 3,882 test samples. Experiments on representative open- and closed-source multimodal large language models reveal substantial and uneven limitations across interaction structures. Models trained on EgoOmniWorld improve in-domain interaction understanding and generalize to existing egocentric benchmarks. EgoOmniWorld provides a unified foundation for evaluating and advancing egocentric video understanding. Data, code, and models will be released.

</details>


### [338] [Dual-Domain Sparse-View 4D CBCT Reconstruction with Spatial-Temporal Consistency Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18518)
*Qi Lai,YuTong He*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Four-dimensional cone-beam computed tomography (4D CBCT) provides phase-resolved volumetric images for image-guided radiotherapy. Yet good reconstruction quality usually needs many projections. This increases scan time and radiation dose. We propose STCNet, a sparse-view 4D CBCT reconstruction framework that uses spatial-temporal consistency learning. STCNet reconstructs high-quality images from only 16 projections in each respiratory phase. STCNet integrates physics-driven positional encoding to enforce geometric consistency in the projection domain and introduces temporal encoding in latent space to capture respiratory motion dynamics across phases to capture dynamic respiratory motion across phases. A dual-domain collaborative strategy jointly optimizes both projection and image domains, enhancing structural fidelity and temporal coherence. We tested STCNet on the TCIA-LUNG dataset and a CIRS thorax phantom dataset. STCNet performed better than conventional methods and recent deep learning baselines in image quality, artifact reduction, and structural preservation. On TCIA-LUNG, STCNet reached PSNR values of 33.311, 32.695, and 32.658 dB at phases 2, 5, and 8. The corresponding SSIM values were 0.845, 0.823, and 0.825. On CIRS, STCNet achieved PSNR values up to 36.497 dB and SSIM values up to 0.877. These results support STCNet as a promising framework for low-dose, phase-resolved 4D CBCT reconstruction in image-guided radiotherapy.

</details>


### [339] [Meta-Video: Efficient Standardized Evaluation for Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18535)
*Soumya Shamarao Jahagirdar,Anna Kukleva,Vishaal Udandarao,Samuel Thomas,Brian Kingsbury,Rogerio Feris,James R. Glass. Ameya Prabhu,Hilde Kuehne*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Video understanding benchmarks are the primary yardstick for measuring progress in video-language modeling, and a growing body of work relies on them to claim improvements. The reported benchmark scores in these works, however, are almost always produced by frameworks (e.g., lmms eval) used as black boxes: certain evaluation choices like frame sampling, preprocessing, and input formatting are inherited from preset defaults and seldom inspected carefully. We show these unexamined choices are a major source of variance. Even for the same model checkpoint, evaluation configuration alone can shift accuracy by up to 19.5pp. Hence, it is unclear whether reported gains reflect genuine improvements in capability or differences in evaluation setup. Moreover, many benchmark samples offer little evidence of visual capability, since they can be answered without the video, and the full suites are expensive to evaluate. We address these issues in two parts. First, we curate existing suites for discriminability and efficiency, producing Meta-Video, a balanced 1,000-question benchmark over 791 videos spanning diverse tasks and durations, which largely preserves model rankings, at 5 times inference speedup for a full-frame count sweep experiment (4-128 frames). Second, we establish a standardized evaluation protocol by sweeping the configuration space to isolate choices that measurably affect results, distill them into best practices and reporting standards, and release reproducible reference scores across five models and seven benchmarks. We hope that the proposed evaluation framework and the Meta-Video benchmark will support video understanding research by providing a reproducible and efficient foundation to measure progress in a reliable way.

</details>


### [340] [Beyond the Encoder: Object-Centric Representations Emerging from Segmentation Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18543)
*Michalis Pistos,Bartłomiej W. Papież*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Foundation models are increasingly used as feature extractors for downstream tasks with limited data, typically through representations obtained from their encoders. However, foundation models trained for structured tasks such as segmentation also form object-specific representations during task execution, whose transferability remains largely unexplored. We investigate Segment Anything Model 2 (SAM2) object pointers, compact object-level representations produced through the segmentation pathway, as transferable representations for downstream tumour prediction. Through experiments on two PET/CT tumour prediction tasks, we find that object pointers learned using segmentation supervision alone already encode tumour-discriminative information, despite never being optimised for the downstream labels. We further introduce task-guided object pointers, where an auxiliary objective jointly shapes the object pointer space for the downstream prediction together with segmentation. Task-guided pointers improve cancer-type classification, outperforming conventional encoder-based transfer and radiomics, while segmentation-only pointers perform best on the more challenging recurrence prediction task. Representation analyses reveal associations between object pointers and interpretable radiomics features, class-dependent organisation of the learned representation space, and explicit spatial grounding around the segmented tumour. Together, these findings suggest that useful transferable representations can emerge through the execution of a foundation model's primary task, providing an alternative to conventional encoder-based transfer.

</details>


### [341] [Denoise X: Self-Supervised U-Net for Quantum Mottle Suppression in Chest Radiography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18556)
*Parth Giramkar,Aryan Patle,Vanita Kshirsagar*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Deep learning for medical image restoration is often constrained by the need for paired training data. Acquiring perfectly registered clean-noisy radiographs in vivo is impractical due to patient motion and radiation-dose limits. To address this, we employ a self-supervised Noise2Noise paradigm that trains on independently corrupted observations without paired clean images. We propose Denoise X, a lightweight self-supervised U-Net model (7.79M parameters) for suppressing quantum mottle and electronic readout noise in low-dose chest radiographs. It is trained on the NIH ChestX-ray14 dataset (112,120 images) using a stochastic Poisson-Gaussian noise simulator. Zero-shot evaluation on the unseen Montgomery County dataset achieved a PSNR of 36.09 dB and SSIM of 0.8806, outperforming the Bilateral filter while preserving high-frequency anatomical details. Using patch-wise inference, Denoise X processes native-resolution chest radiographs in ~2.2 s per image on a Tesla P100 GPU. Cross-domain evaluation on the Shenzhen dataset achieved 36.44 dB PSNR and 0.9099 SSIM, showing strong generalization. For resource-constrained deployment, the model was converted into a 31.16 MB ONNX model for efficient CPU inference on non-DICOM images.

</details>


### [342] [Observation Quality in DSM-Assisted Aerial Localization: A Benchmark and Base-Anchored Uncertainty Calibration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18564)
*Pengyu Yin,Junxiang Tan,Jie Pan,Mengqi Fu,Kun Cheng,Yifan Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Aerial map matching provides position observations for navigation, but a position error alone does not determine how an observation should be weighted. We define five benchmark tasks: position availability and agreement, grouped error structure, predictive uncertainty, controlled updates, and temporal accumulation. A case study in digital-surface-model-assisted localization evaluates base-anchored uncertainty calibration within this framework. The method combines propagated image and map sensitivities with the second moment of a compact configuration subset relative to the reported position. The reported position remains unchanged, and scale is fitted using separate position references without local surface-height ground truth. Thirteen covariance models are evaluated on two held-out query sets from locations represented in the calibration data. Relative to a constant full-matrix baseline, the method reduces equal-location normalized position-update risk by 7.70% on 728 conditions and 5.46% on a separate 142 conditions under an independent prior, with all localization failures included. On the primary set, equal-location negative log likelihood improves by 2.571 nat. These results show improved reference-based weighting at seen locations. Exploratory base-quality and conditional-addition controls leave the incremental update benefit of configuration disagreement unresolved. Navigation benefit and real temporal accumulation remain untested.

</details>


### [343] [Parameter-Efficient Multimodal Brain Tumor Segmentation With Stage-Wise Shared Adapters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18571)
*Milad Rostamian,Mohammadreza Mohammadi,Ender Konukoglu,Mohsen Soryani*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Different MRI modalities reveal different parts of a brain tumor. Segmentation models combine information from these modalities to outline tumor regions. Many models use a separate encoder for each modality. However, exchanging features among these encoders at several stages can increase model complexity. We introduce the Block-Wise Shared Adapter (BWSA), which combines compressed features from the modality encoders in a shared path and returns adapted features to each stream. In its fixed-blending form, BWSA adds the adapted features to the original features. Its trainable-gated variant (TG-BWSA) instead learns separate weights for the original and adapted features at each modality and encoder stage. We evaluate both variants on BraTS 2020 and BraTS 2023 with four segmentation backbones. On the official BraTS 2023 online validation set, BWSA increases average region-wise Dice by 1.3--7.6 percentage points relative to the corresponding unmodified backbones. TG-BWSA increases it by 2.8--7.7 points. Both variants add approximately 0.85--3.73% parameters and reduce average HD95 relative to the unmodified backbones.

</details>


### [344] [Detecting Unreliable Classifications from Quantized Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18575)
*Ananya Kotla,Jorge T. Gomez*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Post-training INT8 quantization is typically validated by checking accuracy retention, which requires ground-truth labels often unavailable in low-resource clinical settings. We show a label-free alternative: comparing a model's own output before and after quantization. We compute cosine similarity between the FP32 and INT8 models' output probability vectors for each prediction, which requires only a single forward pass. This output-agreement similarity separates lower-accuracy predictions across four evaluated architectures (ResNet-50, VGG-16-BN, Wide ResNet-50, and ViT-B/16) under fixed-percentile splits, outperforming a confidence-only baseline for all four. These results show that quantized models can be compared to their baselines to assess their drift and determine whether they are well suited for clinical deployment.

</details>


### [345] [Rethinking Projector Training in Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18598)
*Fabian Gröger,Naël Ouerghemi,Shuo Wen,Maria Brbić*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Multimodal LLMs are typically trained by first aligning a vision encoder with a language model via projector training on large image-caption datasets, followed by joint training of the full multimodal model. Despite its widespread use, the role of the projector training stage remains poorly understood. We find that (i) using only $10$ to $20\%$ of the data for training the projector already recovers most downstream performance gains, and (ii) projectors trained on different datasets can be linearly interpolated while largely preserving performance. These results suggest that projector training mainly performs a coarse alignment, which many different projectors achieve equally well. Motivated by these findings, we propose PORTAL, a training-free projector initialization that requires neither paired image-caption data nor gradient-based optimization, but only per-modality summary statistics. PORTAL computes an optimal-transport-based map between Gaussian approximations of the vision and language feature distributions on a shared principal subspace, so that the projector training stage can be skipped entirely. Without any paired data, PORTAL matches the standard pipeline trained on 558k image-caption pairs across six LLM backbones, two vision encoders, and 16 benchmarks. It outperforms that pipeline in $3$ of $7$ (vision encoder, LLM) settings and reaches an average $98\%$ of its score across the other four.

</details>


### [346] [LLM2R: From Large Language Models to 3D Reassemblers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18604)
*Zhouhan Zhong,Qiuhong Shen,Xingyi Yang,Jinyi Xu,Xinchao Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: 3D part assembly aims to reconstruct a complete shape from a set of unassembled parts by predicting a rigid SE(3) transformation for each part. Because the placement of a part is rarely determined by its own geometry, the task demands reasoning over the entire part set, and existing methods, which rely on pairwise relations or iterative generation and verification, scale poorly to objects with many parts. We present LLM2R, which casts part assembly as sequence modeling with a pretrained decoder-only large language model (LLM): a Point-BERT encoder and a Q-Former compress each part into a block of geometric tokens, the blocks of all parts are concatenated, and a causal LLM adapted with Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) processes the sequence in one forward pass. Causal attention, however, lets the hidden states of a part observe only the parts that precede it. LLM2R therefore appends a block of learnable summary tokens after the last part: these tokens follow every part, so their states aggregate the whole assembly at a constant token cost, and a part-aware pose head fuses each part's states with them before predicting its rotation and translation. One architecture thus handles semantic and geometric assembly and predicts all poses in a single non-autoregressive pass. LLM2R achieves the highest part accuracy and success rate on all three PartNet categories, the best rotation error, translation error, and part accuracy on PartNeXt, and the lowest translation error and fastest inference on Breaking Bad, where only a fracture-specialized method is more accurate. Its advantage grows with the number of parts: at 100 parts it exceeds GARF by 33.5 points in part accuracy on Breaking Bad and Assembler by 29.1 points on PartNet, and it transfers zero-shot to unseen categories with more than 16 points higher part accuracy than the strongest of three baselines.

</details>


### [347] [Recasting DiT for Extreme Image Compression with Hierarchical Visual Priors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18607)
*Yunuo Chen,Jingnan Gao,Bing He,Jinjing Zhao,Chuqin Zhou,Xiaoyue Ling,Li Song,Guo Lu,Wenjun Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Pretrained diffusion models provide powerful generative priors for extreme image compression, but their stochastic, iterative generation processes are not designed to decode compressed representations directly. We present PrismCodec, which recasts a pretrained Diffusion Transformer (DiT) as a bitstream-grounded one-step generative decoder. PrismCodec uses the decoded representation in two complementary roles: a projected codec state defines the starting state for generation, while hierarchical visual priors derived from the same decoded latent provide semantic and structural guidance. To bridge the gap between stochastic, iterative generation and deterministic one-step decoding, we develop a progressive adaptation strategy. We first learn codec-conditioned transport from a noisy neighborhood centered on the codec state, distill it into a one-step mapping, and finally anneal the source perturbation to zero during end-to-end optimization. The resulting decoder reconstructs deterministically from the received representation without additional conditioning bits. Across Kodak, CLIC, and DIV2K, PrismCodec consistently improves rate-perception performance over prior generative codecs, achieving BD-rate reductions of 70-75% in LPIPS and 75-81% in DISTS over StableCodec. Code will be released upon acceptance.

</details>


### [348] [VideoJev: System One Model for Video Understanding and Interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18615)
*Yuhan Zhu,Xiangyu Zeng,Qingyi Si,Chenxu Yang,Yifei Li,Luozheng Qin,Junhao Zhou,Dingyu Yao,Jun Zhang,Chuanyu Qin,Zhiqiu Zhang,Yuandong Yang,Yuqi Xu,Tianyu Zhang,Hongcheng Guo,Jiaqi Wang,Nan Duan,Limin Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Visual agents must turn video observations into timely decisions to track progress and choose what to do next. Building on the emerging System One paradigm, we introduce VideoJev, a family of models that cast video understanding and interaction as structured decision tasks. We adapt pretrained multimodal large language models (MLLMs) by replacing autoregressive answer decoding with a shared head that scores temporal intervals and textual options against video evidence. These scores yield event spans or answer choices, unifying temporal grounding and multiple-choice video question answering. This combines the semantic flexibility of MLLMs with efficient decisions that agents can use directly. As an interaction unfolds, new goals and intermediate results prompt further queries about the same visual history. VideoJev therefore reuses visual computation through Cached inference for queries as they arrive and Joint inference for multiple queries in a single forward pass. Across three temporal grounding benchmarks, VideoJev-4B and VideoJev-35B-A3B improve mean mIoU over their backbones by 6.7 and 10.8 points, respectively, with measured inference speedups of $2.8$-$3.1\times$. On four video QA benchmarks, both achieve performance comparable to their backbones in Thinking mode, with measured inference speedups exceeding $100\times$. These results offer a unified, reusable decision interface for composing structured visual judgments into complex, open-ended workflows.

</details>


### [349] [MajorTOM:Elliot Pretrain, A Multi-Sensor, Multi-Temporal Pretraining Dataset for Earth Observation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18624)
*Carmen Oliver Huidobro,Mikolaj Czerkawski,Kevin Monsalvez-Pozo,Cesar Aybar,Gustau Camps-Valls,Oscar J. Pellicer-Valero*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Pretraining generalist Earth observation foundation models requires data that combine several sensors, varied temporal sampling and, increasingly, text, yet open datasets rarely provide all three in one consistent, easily accessible form. We introduce MajorTOM:Elliot Pretrain, a collection of 279,166 tiles of 10 km drawn from the Major-TOM grid by clustering AlphaEarth embeddings. Each tile stacks Sentinel-2 L1C, Landsat-8/9, Sentinel-1 RTC, Copernicus DEM and ESA WorldCover on a shared 1056x1056-pixel, 10 m grid, complemented by cloud masks, OpenStreetMap entities and ERA5 weather. The tiles are partitioned into three temporal regimes, Monotemporal (250,000 tiles, one acquisition), Monthly (12,500 tiles, twelve acquisitions spread over the annual cycle) and Burst (16,666 tiles, six acquisitions within 30 days), and are released as 6.3 TB of Cloud Optimized GeoTIFFs indexed by TACO v3 Parquet metadata, a standard that allows cloud streaming, efficient data access, and filtering before any pixel is read. A companion library derives sensor-tagged fact sheets, captions and 16 families of vision-language tasks from these layers at load time. We test our design choices in short pretraining runs of OlmoEarth-Base. First, a 25% subset of Major-TOM Core-S2L2A curated with AlphaEarth embeddings matches or exceeds the full corpus on 8 of 9 benchmarks. Second, on Elliot-Pretrain, adding Landsat-8/9 improves or matches Sentinel-2 only pretraining on every benchmark. Third, using DEM and WorldCover as prediction targets during pretraining further improves 8 of 9 benchmarks, while giving the same maps to the encoder as inputs degrades 5 of 9. Fourth, Sentinel-1 helps on 7 of 9 benchmarks, particularly on benchmarks with radar inputs. Fifth, rule-based captions match a small language model at no cost. The dataset is openly available at https://source.coop/major-tom/elliot-pretrain.

</details>


### [350] [ST-GRU PredRNN-V2: A GRU-Based Reformulation of PredRNN-V2 for Video Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18645)
*Augusta van Haren,Hanna Hoogen,Luca Pattavina,Quincy Rommen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Video prediction requires models to capture complex spatiotemporal dependencies and to retain relevant information over long sequences. PredRNN and its successor PredRNN-V2 address this challenge with spatiotemporal Long Short-Term Memory (ST-LSTM) units that maintain two separate memory states, at the cost of a relatively complex recurrent building block. In this work, we investigate whether the simpler gating of Gated Recurrent Units (GRUs) can be transferred to spatiotemporal predictive learning. To this end, we develop a spatiotemporal GRU (ST-GRU) unit as a GRU-based alternative to the ST-LSTM. The ST-GRU replaces the ST-LSTM's three gates per memory state with two GRU-style gates (update and reset), and merges the temporal memory state into the hidden state, which reduces the number of parameters by about 5%. We incorporate the ST-GRU into PredRNN-V2 and evaluate the resulting model on the Moving MNIST video prediction benchmark, following the original experimental protocol. Averaged over ten predicted frames, the ST-GRU-based PredRNN-V2 achieves an MSE of 55.1, an SSIM of 0.883, an LPIPS of 0.065 and a PSNR of 19.53dB. It does not surpass the original ST-LSTM-based PredRNN-V2 on MSE, SSIM or PSNR, but it achieves a lower LPIPS and performs on par with the earlier ST-LSTM-based PredRNN, while using a simpler recurrent cell. These results suggest that ST-GRUs are a promising building block for spatiotemporal predictive learning and motivate further work on their design and integration.

</details>


### [351] [Texture Interpolation as Texton Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18655)
*Ido Madar,Gal Oren,Shai Avidan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Texture interpolation asks what should lie between two textures. Standard methods typically blend pixels or features at corresponding spatial locations, implicitly assuming that co-located elements should be mixed. For unrelated textures, however, this correspondence is arbitrary: a crack at one location has no particular relationship to a fiber at the same location in another image. We study whether this hidden pairing choice is itself a major determinant of interpolation quality. We represent textures as collections of local appearance vectors, or textons, and replace positional pairing with appearance-aware matching while keeping the encoder, representation, and decoder fixed. This isolates the effect of correspondence from changes in model capacity or training. Across texture pairs, appearance-aware matching consistently improves the distributional quality of interpolated midpoints over positional feature blending. Soft matching produces the strongest midpoint statistics, whereas one-to-one assignments yield more balanced transitions and closer agreement with the target endpoint. The same intervention also improves a second pretrained texture interpolator without retraining. These results identify correspondence as a first-class design choice in texture interpolation: meaningful transitions depend not only on how strongly two textures are mixed, but also on which local appearances are mixed with one another.

</details>


### [352] [PlanPath: Exploration-Guided Orchestration for Patient-Level Pathology Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18664)
*Jiusong Ge,Mingzhao He,Qidong Liu,Chunbao Wang,Chunze Yang,Di Zhang,Jiashuai Liu,Mireia Crispin-Ortuzar,Huazhu Fu,Chen Li,Zeyu Gao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: A patient's pathology case spans tens of slides from distinct specimens and anatomical sites, each carrying non-redundant, site-specific evidence. Forming a patient-level conclusion therefore requires locating the slides relevant to a given question and integrating complementary findings across them. However, existing pathology agents analyze one slide at a time and describe morphology rather than measure it, leaving evidence acquisition across a patient's specimens and patient-level quantification underexplored. To address this gap, we propose PlanPath, an agentic framework for patient-level pathology visual question answering (VQA). PlanPath turns the question into an exploration plan that specifies the evidence required, routes to the slides likely to carry it, and drives coarse-to-fine acquisition from tissue architecture down to individual cells. Tissue- and cell-level tools supply the counts, proportions, and extents, which PlanPath aggregates across slides into a traceable answer. To enable evaluation, we build two patient-level VQA datasets for renal and gastric cancer, averaging 15 WSIs per patient, with pathologist-verified question-answer pairs derived from diagnostic reports. Experimental results show that PlanPath outperforms the strongest baseline by 9.4% and 12.1% in overall accuracy on the two datasets and further improves existing slide-level agents when plugged into our framework. The dataset and code are available at: https://github.com/JSGe-AI/PlanPath_1.

</details>


### [353] [RadarGSOcc: Doppler-Aware 3D Semantic Occupancy Prediction Using 4D Radar Tensor with Geometric-Semantic Feature Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18686)
*Christofel Rio Goenawan,Dong-Hee Paek,Seung-Hyun Kong*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: 3D semantic occupancy prediction is crucial for autonomous driving, but camera-based methods degrade in low-light conditions and LiDAR-based methods are sensitive to adverse weather. 4D radar is more robust, yet its sparsity and the high cost of radar tensor processing limit its use. We propose Radar Geometric-Semantic Occupancy Prediction (RadarGSOcc), an efficient framework that directly processes 4D radar tensors by separately extracting geometric and semantic features, including Doppler information, with a deformable attention-based geometric refinement module as its key component. We also introduce the K-Radar 3DSemOcc benchmark, the first large-scale benchmark for 3D semantic occupancy prediction using 4D radar tensors, built on the K-Radar dataset with refined LiDAR-based annotations. RadarGSOcc predicts 15 classes within a 51.2m range and achieves 38.2% IoU, 7.6% mIoU, and 25.5% weighted mIoU, outperforming RadarOcc by 17% relative (25.5 vs. 21.9) in weighted mIoU while using fewer parameters and lower computation. It substantially improves truck prediction while trading off car accuracy, outperforms camera-based approaches, and narrows the gap with LiDAR-based methods. To assess robustness without per-frame ground truth in adverse weather, we further propose a novel proxy metric, Environment-Based KL Divergence (EBKL), under which 4D radar proves a reliable and efficient modality for 3D semantic occupancy prediction. The dataset and code will be publicly available.

</details>


### [354] [RSIgen: Reciprocal Self-Improvement for Agentic Visual Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18688)
*Yidi Liu,Haozhe Wang,Jie Xiao,Kai Zhu,Xueyang Fu,Zheng-Jun Zha*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Agentic visual generation produces experience that can improve the native understanding (agent) and generation roles of a unified multimodal backbone, but task success alone does not determine which role should learn. Generator updates can also make the agent's action supervision stale. We introduce RSIgen, a task-centric reciprocal self-improvement framework linking learning allocation, supervision refresh, and recursive curriculum construction. Dynamic Responsibility Routing (DRR) uses matched interventions on evidence, inputs, and supported actions to select learning recipients and construct compatible training targets. After generator learning, RSIgen re-evaluates the relative utility of tested actions with branch inputs held fixed to refresh agent supervision; the retained roles then construct the next curriculum. Across BAGEL, Qwen-Image, and Wan-Image-7B, RSIgen improves AgentGen-Bench Full Overall9 by 3.2--3.3 points over a three-cycle SearchGen adaptation with action costs. Wan controls support allocation gains at matched role-level training exposure, refresh gains at a matched adaptation budget, and benefits from updated curriculum sources while recipients continue learning. In blind evaluation on 200 real-user requests, pooled tie-adjusted preference scores against the same baseline reach 70.0% for task completion and 64.9% for visual quality.

</details>


### [355] [Just Respect the Semantics: Image-Token Compression via Pre-LLM Semantic Variable-Resolution Tokenization and In-LLM Relevance Pruning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18690)
*Tianhao Fu,Austin Wang,Charles Chen,Roby Aldave-Garza,Victor Xie,Xiyue Jiao,Yucheng Chen,Martin Tin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Image-token compression must reduce computation without hurting the semantics. We introduce Varest, guided by semantic fungibility: tokens within a coherent semantic region can be summarized coarsely, while object boundaries and literal text require finer distinctions. Object and text localizers guide variable-resolution merging, preserving spatial coverage and finer detail at boundaries and text. Learned [REL] registers then prune visual tokens after early decoder processing. Training across budgets enables one checkpoint per backbone. Across three backbones and 13 benchmarks, Varest outperforms 15 baselines in mean performance retention at every evaluated budget, retaining 86.10-92.33% of full-token performance with only 5.56% of the original layer-averaged image-token count.

</details>


### [356] [Orientation Estimation of Cryo-EM Images for Molecules with Arbitrary Symmetry by Semidefinite Programming](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18691)
*Yunpeng Shi,Amit Singer,Jinxin Wang,Ruiyi Yang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: This paper extends the non-unique games framework introduced in Bandeira et al. (2020) to address symmetric structures. Precisely, we consider the problem \[ \begin{aligned}
  \underset{g_1,\ldots,g_n\in \mathcal{G}}{\operatorname{min}}\,\, \frac{1}{|S|}\sum_{i,j=1}^n \sum_{s\in S} f_{ij}(g_i s g_j^{-1}), \end{aligned} \] where $\mathcal{G}$ is a compact group, $S\subset \mathcal{G}$ is a finite subgroup of symmetry elements, and $f_{ij}:\mathcal{G}\rightarrow \mathbb{R}$ is a loss function. A notable example is common lines based orientation estimation of cryo-EM images for molecules with point group symmetry $S$, where $\mathcal{G}$ is the rotation group $\operatorname{SO}(3)$. With a semidefinite programming relaxation, we turn the problem into a convex program that is solved with the alternating direction method of multipliers. Unlike existing approaches which are tailored to specific symmetry groups, ours provides a unified computational framework for addressing all point group symmetries. We demonstrate the applicability of the proposed algorithm on the cryo-EM orientation estimation problem on both simulated and experimental datasets. In particular, our algorithm is effective in generating high-quality ab initio models from only a few class averages computed from experimental images.

</details>


### [357] [GeoReader: Learning Latent Geometry for Spatial Reasoning in Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18694)
*Rong Li,Shijie Li,Jiangyong Huang,Lingdong Kong,Jiaming Zhou,Dongyue Lu,Zeying Gong,Baoxiong Jia,Junwei Liang,Siyuan Huang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Vision-language models (VLMs) struggle with metric and multi-view relationships that remain implicit in RGB observations. Geometry foundation models recover this structure from RGB, but existing methods either fuse the estimated features with visual tokens and learn to use them from question answering alone, or align VLM latents with geometry features. Capturing geometry, however, does not ensure its use: zeroing the aligned latents of a released feature-alignment model leaves its accuracy unchanged. We introduce GeoReader, a framework that learns geometric representations for the VLM that reads them. First, we train lightweight Geometric Readers to produce continuous input tokens, supervising them with geometry descriptions decoded by a frozen VLM, so that the Readers express geometry in a form the target model can already read. We then freeze the Readers to preserve this interface while fine-tuning the VLM on spatial QA, with geometry injection conditioned on the question. Geometry descriptions serve only as training targets: at inference, the VLM answers directly from the continuous geometric tokens without generating a geometry chain of thought. With geometry predicted from RGB images, our approach reaches 94.3 on MindCube-Tiny with Qwen3-VL-4B and, with a multi-source QA mixture, an average of 72.5 across six additional spatial benchmarks with Qwen3.5-9B. These results suggest that a useful geometric representation is one the receiving model can use, not merely one from which geometry can be reconstructed.

</details>


### [358] [CryoTracer: A Synthetic-to-Real System for Filament Instance Tracing in Cryo-EM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18696)
*Radosław Kuczbański,Wojciech Kozłowski,Mateusz Grzesiuk,Konrad Karanowski,Lukas Frey,Andrzej Rzepiela,Maciej Zięba*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Particle picking in cryo-electron microscopy is commonly formulated as an object-detection problem in which individual particles are represented by bounding boxes, a natural representation for globular particles but a poor match for filaments that can extend across an entire micrograph. Existing methods reconstruct filament trajectories by linking local predictions in a separate post-processing stage, decoupling detection from instance formation and creating potential ambiguities at gaps and crossings. Directly preserving filament identity is particularly useful for polymorphic specimens, where segments originating from the same filament should remain associated. However, learning such instance-level representations is challenging because expert centerline annotations are scarce and costly to obtain. We introduce CryoTracer, a synthetic-to-real framework that addresses this limitation through procedural micrograph generation, transformer-based polyline prediction, and specimen-specific fine-tuning. Procedural pretraining exposes the model to diverse filament geometries, intersections, and imaging conditions before adaptation using a limited number of fully annotated experimental micrographs. CryoTracer represents filaments as ordered centerline polylines and optimizes their geometry using an orientation-invariant discrete Fréchet objective, enabling consistent learning regardless of tracing direction. Evaluation on established and newly expert-annotated datasets shows that CryoTracer improves instance-level filament recovery over existing particle-detection approaches. These results establish end-to-end polyline prediction as a practical alternative to detection-and-linking pipelines for recovering complete filament instances with limited micrograph annotation budgets. Code and data are available at https://github.com/cryotracer/cryotracer

</details>


### [359] [AIRRI: Towards a Human-Readable, Machine-Resistant Reading Interface](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18724)
*Emory Salaun,Syed Ayon,Kaleel Mahmood,Shaun Wallace*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: The rapid advancement of image-to-text extraction systems and large language models (LLMs) poses new challenges for educational assessment by making assignment materials easy to transcribe and solve with minimal human effort. To address this problem, we present AIRRI (Artificial Intelligence Resilient Reading Interface), a framework for generating machine-resistant educational content, a dataset of higher-education assignments for evaluation, and an initial evaluation of the human readability of AIRRI machine-resistant text designs. AIRRI employs two categories of machine-resistance techniques. First, AIRRI applies image transformations including character spacing, word spacing, line spacing, character opacity variation, stripe overlays, and image noise. Second, AIRRI applies decision-based adversarial attacks: RayS, SurFree, and ADBA. We evaluate AIRRI against three image-to-text systems: Tesseract, EasyOCR, and the transformer-based GOT-OCR2 model. Our results show that AIRRI substantially degrades automated text extraction performance. AIRRI increased character error rates for EasyOCR (6.9% to 55.9%), Tesseract (2.3% to 100.0%), and GOT-OCR2 (30.7% to 48.1%). Word error rates increased for EasyOCR (24.9% to 96.7%) and Tesseract (10.7% to 100.0%). Among the evaluated decision-based attacks, ADBA consistently produced the largest degradation in image-to-text performance, while RayS and SurFree produced more moderate reductions. EasyOCR and Tesseract were generally more susceptible to both image-based and adversarial perturbations than the transformer-based GOT-OCR2 model. Our findings suggest that AIRRI can significantly reduce the reliability of image-to-text-to-LLM workflows, increasing the effort required for AI-assisted assignment completion.

</details>


### [360] [Segment Where You Look: Attention as the Interface Between Language Models and Segmenters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18729)
*Eslam Abdelrahman,Hamza Omar Alkaf,Yuheng Li,Siddharth Srinivasan Iyer,Nanxuan Zhao,Eli Shechtman,Krishna Kumar Singh,Mohamed Elhoseiny*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Vision-language models (VLMs) that segment from language must pass the location of the referent to a pixel decoder. The dominant interface compresses it into the hidden state of a single [SEG] token, a d-dimensional vector with no spatial index, and leaves the decoder, which never sees the expression, to recover the referent's extent. We show that the VLM already computes a better signal: the [SEG] token's attention over the image tokens, which is defined on the patch grid by construction. Probing a frozen segmentation VLM, we find that this attention yields 18 to 22 more gIoU than the [SEG] hidden state when read by the same probe, that it gains nothing from the image where the hidden state does, and that it is concentrated in the late layers. Building on these findings, we introduce AttnSeg, which replaces the [SEG] embedding with this attention as the decoder's prompt. A learned, signed combination of the attention heads forms a dense spatial prompt, and an attention-guidance objective supervises it directly. A two-stage recipe first trains the VLM to localize without the decoder, then trains the decoder to refine, within the budget of standard fine-tuning. Applied to Sa2VA, AttnSeg improves ReasonSeg by 6.4 gIoU and GS-Eval by 10.2 at 4B (4.2 and 9.6 over the same model fine-tuned on the same data), and by 8.8 and 11.3 at 14B. The gains reach 13 to 18 gIoU on object parts, multiple objects, and single objects, where the referent must be singled out, and are negligible on stuff regions, which the decoder already recovers unaided. Code and models are available at https://eslambakr.github.io/attseg_project_page/

</details>


### [361] [Neural Block-wise Auto-exposure for Single-Shot High Dynamic Range Imaging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18733)
*Zhengguo Li,Chaobing Zheng,Wei Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Block-wise varying exposures are effective for cap- turing appearance of a real-world high dynamic range (HDR) scene via a single-shot. However, brightness order reversal (BOR) artifacts, blocking artifacts and loss of details in highlight/shadow regions are possible issues for block-wise varying exposures. In this paper, a novel neural block-wise auto-exposure algorithm is proposed to address the issues. The proposed algorithm maps a temporary image and its exposure time to the exposure times of all non-overlapping 16 X 16 blocks. The temporary image can be either an sRGB or a raw image. Although the sRGB image is widely adopted in existing auto-exposure algorithms, exposure times of blocks in the highlight and shadow regions might not be accurately learned due to the limited radiance measurement capability of the sRGB image. This paper thus also explores the feasibility of temporary image as a raw image. Due to the better radiance measurement capability of the raw image, the exposure times of blocks are learned more accurately, and more details in the highlight/shadow regions of the real-world HDR scene are captured by the resultant HDR imaging system. Experiment results validate the proposed neural block-wise auto-exposure algorithm.

</details>


### [362] [Concealed Handgun Detection using WiFi Signals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18736)
*Erik Persson,David Gustafsson,Tomas Wilkinson*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: While conventional camera-based surveillance systems and metal detectors can provide a sufficient level of security when used appropriately, they have fundamental limitations that reduce their applicability. Camera-based systems are unable to detect occluded or concealed threats and metal detectors require each person to enter through a narrow opening one at a time, leading to congestion issues and limited use in large crowds. A WiFi-based security system has the potential to be a complementary modality that is able to handle occlusions or concealment and is not hindered by strict spatial configurations. In this paper, we propose use Channel State Information estimated from WiFi-signals to detect concealed handguns. To this end we collect a dataset of aligned WiFi signals and RGB images that we call the WiFire dataset. We also propose a deep learning model called WiFireNet tailored to detect concealed handguns in WiFi signals using vision-based supervision signals. Our results show that concealed handgun detection is possible in WiFi and has promise as a complementary modality to surveillance cameras and metal detectors. We release the dataset, model and code to facilitate the further research into WiFi-based handgun detection.

</details>


### [363] [Context-aware Bayesian Fusion for Multimodal Object Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18741)
*Arunachalam Venkatachalam,Mhafuzul Islam,Marwan Yusuf,David Van Hamme,Ben Stoffelen,James Foresi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Reliable detection of pedestrians and road users is essential for the safe operation of autonomous vehicles. However, this task becomes increasingly challenging under adverse environmental conditions, such as poor weather and low lighting, where the performance of state-of-the-art pre-trained object detectors and on-board sensors tends to degrade, which limits their effectiveness and reliability. Moreover, retraining models for each operating context imposes significant constraints due to high data requirements, computational resources, and time. Considering this, we propose a novel context-aware multimodal late fusion approach that integrates a lightweight gating network within our probabilistic calibration framework. The gating network extracts environmental context and dynamically modulates the detection outputs of pre-trained object detectors based on the inferred context, without retraining of the detectors. Our methodology is evaluated on a modified KITTI dataset, in which the camera images and lidar pointclouds are augmented for varying fog levels. Experimental results show that our approach yields an approximately 14% relative improvement over non-adaptive late fusion baselines, achieving a consistent average precision of 56% across different fog levels.

</details>


### [364] [LayerCanvas: Infographic Generation in AI Learning Assistants via Hybrid Layer-Set Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18763)
*Keshav Gupta,Jeremy Owen,Christopher Lloyd,Shaunak Mishra,Manav Sharma*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Online learning platforms are increasingly deploying AI assistants to provide personalized, on-demand tutoring. These can leverage infographics to communicate complex information and improve the learning experience. However, automating effective infographic generation remains a challenging task, requiring a combination of visual richness with precise typography, structured layouts, and continued editability. Existing representation methods satisfy these goals only in part: Scalable Vector Graphics (SVG) representations preserve geometry, hierarchy, and text but provide limited aesthetic flexibility, while raster diffusion models produce richer appearances by flattening content and layout into a single image. Conditioning a diffusion model on a rasterized SVG does not resolve this conflict because the raster preserves approximate placement but discards element ownership, occlusion relationships, and the structure needed to modify and recompose the result. We introduce LayerCanvas, a hybrid approach that retains SVG as the semantic, structural, and typographic program while using diffusion to aestheticize non-text content. Rather than generating one flattened raster, LayerCanvas partitions SVG elements into z-ordered sets of mutually non-overlapping elements and processes them through communicating parallel RGB generation streams. The resulting layers are independently editable and deterministically recomposed, while text is rendered from preserved vector metadata. Our approach combines rich visual synthesis with accurate typography and localized editing. LayerCanvas substantially improves text fidelity over state-of-the-art raster image generators while achieving the highest overall aesthetic quality. We further demonstrate non-destructive content editing, localization, asset reuse, and animation.

</details>


### [365] [MIST: Multi-Scale Spatial Context for Gene Expression Prediction from Histology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18769)
*Koushik Howlader,Tirtho Roy,Md Tauhidul Islam,Wei Le*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Predicting spatial gene expression from standard H&E images could extend molecular analysis to tissues without spatial transcriptomic measurements. A standard Transformer applies self-attention over spots but ignores where each spot sits in the tissue, whereas a spot's molecular state depends on tissue context at multiple spatial scales. Building on this insight, we introduce MIST, a Multi-context Spatial Transformer that augments the self-attention backbone with two context pathways applied in every layer: a coordinate-invariant local pathway that attends over spatial k-nearest-neighbor spots, and a slide pathway that broadcasts a whole-slide summary to every spot; the model predicts all spots in a single forward pass. Because coordinates enter only through pairwise distances, the local pathway is invariant to translations, rotations, and reflections when image features are fixed. In intra-cohort evaluation across six human organ cohorts from HEST-1k, MIST improves mean Pearson correlation over the strongest evaluated baseline by 9.7%, 11.6%, and 11.4% for 10-, 50-, and 100-gene panels, respectively, with the lowest average MSE and MAE. MIST further improves leave-one-organ-out transfer by 6.0% over the next strongest baseline, attains the best expression-derived domain recovery (highest mean ARI and NMI at every clustering resolution, and higher NMI than the closest baseline on 84% of held-out slides), and reproduces spatial expression patterns of selected cancer-marker genes. Together, these results show that adding local and slide context to a vanilla Transformer backbone is an effective approach to spatial gene expression prediction from histology.

</details>


### [366] [The Federated Tumor Segmentation and Classification (FeTS++) Challenge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18771)
*Wenyi Tang,Carla Pitarch-Abaigar,Siddhesh Thakur,Verena Chung,Konstantinos Koukoutegos,Alexandros Karargyris,Holger R. Roth,Spyridon Bakas*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: International challenges have become the standard for developing and benchmarking biomedical image analysis methods using centralized multi-institutional data. Federated learning (FL) describes a paradigm-shift on multi-institutional collaborations, where models are trained across institutions while their data are always retained locally. The Federated Tumor Segmentation (FeTS) challenge, first held in 2021, was the first challenge dedicated to FL in medical imaging and editions in 2022 and 2024 pushed the frontiers of real-world method validation. FeTS++ 2027 returns after a two-year gap with a substantially larger and more diverse cohort, and with the two "pluses" that name the challenge: an official classification task alongside segmentation, and a move of the infrastructure to NVIDIA FLARE (NVFlare). Built on the clinically acquired, multi-institutional multi-parametric MRI data of the BraTS 2025 Lighthouse challenge, FeTS++ spans gliomas and meningiomas across patient cohorts from institutions in North America, Europe, Asia, and Africa, and focuses on ranking submitted methodological approaches according to their model utility and an efficiency-aware score. Task 1 asks participants to find the best strategy for aggregating knowledge from per-institution segmentation models, while Task 2 asks them to distinguish glioma from meningioma under the same federated protocol, in which the entity label is heavily confounded with the site of origin.

</details>


### [367] [GAD: Geometry Attention Distillation for Consistent Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18779)
*Junfeng Ni,Ziyu Zhu,Fan Jiang,Yu Liu,Chiyu Wang,Jiawei Zhang,Zhaoxu Sun,Mu Xu,Siyuan Huang,Yixin Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Video diffusion models generate visually compelling short-term sequences but often struggle to preserve scene geometry and object structure over time. In this paper, we first demonstrate that current video attention cannot identify reliable correspondences across frames, with responses dispersed across multiple regions even after recognizable scene structure has emerged. We argue that reliable cross-frame associations are essential for information exchange as scene structure forms in video generation. Thus, we introduce Geometry Attention Distillation (GAD), which transfers correspondences from a frozen geometry model's attention to video attention. Extracted from clean videos, these correspondences link the same static surface or moving object across frames. Since structure formation begins in deeper layers at higher noise and shallower layers at lower noise, we apply attention distillation at a student layer selected according to the noise timestep. After training, video attention focuses more consistently on corresponding locations. Experiments across all three video backbones demonstrate improved long-range scene consistency and dynamic temporal consistency, with geometric gains across generation lengths. On Wan2.2 5B, GAD improves cross-frame matching accuracy by at least 30% over the Wan baseline on both long-range benchmarks. Notably, GAD achieves these gains without additional inference modules, offering a promising approach to efficient and consistent video-based world simulation. Project page: https://fantasy-amap.github.io/GAD/.

</details>


### [368] [Trust Before Autonomy: Rethinking Agentic Remote Sensing for Crisis Response](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18789)
*Georgios Angelidis,Aggelos Psiris,Spyridon Evangelatos,Thomas Lagkas,Panagiotis Sarigiannidis,Iraklis Varlamis,Georgios Th. Papadopoulos*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Agentic remote sensing is evaluated on how many questions it can answer, whereas crisis response depends on whether its answers can be relied on and how much of the decision can safely be handed to the agent. This paper argues that \emph{autonomy should be earned, not assumed}: An Earth-Observation (EO) agent should be granted autonomy in proportion to demonstrated reliability, epistemic honesty and verifiability. The position is supported by an evidential meta-analysis of twelve agentic RS/EO/GIS benchmark studies from peer-reviewed venues. Only one repeats runs, three contain unanswerable tasks and none measures calibration. Their own tables show that completed workflows are often not correct (task success exceeds tool-call correctness by up to 54pp on identical queries), that the gap to an expert trajectory widens overall with trajectory length (from 7 to 56pp), and that capability does not predict abstention (Spearman r from -0.25 to 0.50, all n.s.). The paper then contributes three EO-specific trust properties, an autonomy ladder for crisis EO tasks, and a readiness protocol that replays archived emergency activations before any live use. In this context, current evidence justifies assistive roles only.

</details>


### [369] [Registration is a Representation Problem: Solving Whole-Body PET/CT Registration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18804)
*Neel Dey,Adrian V. Dalca*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Raw image intensities are a poor surrogate for similarity across images in correspondence problems. Further, while auxiliary segmentation labels can provide semantic identification and improve matching, they can assign only coarse identities to fine-grained anatomy and often cover only a subset of structures, particularly in whole-body settings. We present an approach that instead establishes correspondence using complementary representations of anatomy and demonstrate its utility for longitudinal whole-body PET/CT registration in the Learn2Reg 2026 PSMAReg Challenge. Our 3D foundation model, anatomix, is used to derive fine-grained, per-voxel semantic representations that are consistent across volumes, and we further employ off-the-shelf segmentation models to obtain coarse anatomical identities. Together, these representations drive a GPU-accelerated iterative registration solver (FireANTs) in a hybrid multistage framework: the first stage estimates alignment from dense multi-channel features, after which a second refines the deformation using both anatomix- and segmentation-based losses. Lastly, because registration should not introduce non-biological tumor volume changes across time, we develop lesion-specific component-wise volume preservation schemes. Without any task-specific training, our system won first place in the PSMAReg Challenge leaderboard. Code and interactive visualizations are available at http://www.neeldey.com/reps-for-reg.

</details>


### [370] [DuplexBridge: Coordinating Understanding and Generation for Unified Image Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18819)
*Yidi Liu,Jie Xiao,Chaojie Mao,Jingfeng Zhang,Xueyang Fu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: Complex image editing requires faithful task interpretation and accurate execution, but a failed edit does not reveal which responsibility failed. We present DuplexBridge, which uses a unified model's own understanding capability to enhance prompts for its own generation capability, without an external understanding module. A source-grounded bridge specifies targets, final-state changes, and preservation constraints. Understanding is supervised on fidelity to the original request without observing the generated image; generation is supervised on execution of the condition it actually receives. Reciprocal trust checks selectively admit global task feedback to each role while retaining independently valid local supervision. We introduce CE-Bench, a 1,200-task benchmark measuring joint satisfaction of task-derived change and preservation requirements. Across three model configurations, the full system improves complete compliance over direct editors by 21.75-25.75 percentage points, with gains also observed on KRIS-Bench, RISEBench, ImgEdit, and GEdit-Bench. From a common supervised Wan-Image initialization, gated joint training reaches 77.08% complete compliance, compared with 70.92% without gates and 75.00% when generation-local supervision instead checks the original task with both gates retained. These results support distinct local responsibilities and selective shared feedback for joint understanding-generation adaptation.

</details>


### [371] [OmniCompass: A Holistic Benchmark for Omni-Modal Video Grounding and Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18829)
*Xuyang Liu,Shihao Wang,Xinyu Wei,Minghan Li,Zeqing Wang,Zuhao Yang,Zichen Wen,Lei Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: N/A


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: N/A

Method: N/A

Result: N/A

Conclusion: N/A

Abstract: With the rapid development of multimodal large language models (MLLMs), evaluating their video understanding capabilities has become increasingly important. Existing video understanding benchmarks cover only part of the evaluation dimensions, limiting systematic diagnosis of omni-modal video understanding.
